```
{"id":"SPI-151","title":"Worker silently dies on transient network failure during Step 2 retrieval","description":"## Problem\n\nWhen the network drops during Step 2 (research & collect PDF sources), the worker process hangs or dies silently. The job record in Postgres remains in `running` state with an expired lease, no `finished_at`, and no `error_message`. From the user's perspective, the run just disappears.\n\n## Observed behavior (2026-04-10)\n\n* Run `20260410T085247Z_api_349683c2` for question `gjopen_q_4773` (US GDP Q2 2026)\n* Step 1 completed successfully (0.87s)\n* Step 2 started, domain profile LLM call succeeded\n* At 09:56:06 all outbound HTTP requests began failing with `SSLError` (World Bank, OpenAlex, NewsAPI, ECB, Federal Reserve, FRED) — consistent with a full network drop\n* Worker log stopped writing at 09:56:16\n* Worker process (PID 28356) remained alive but produced no further output\n* Job lease expired at 09:02:48 — no lease renewal, no error written back\n* `evidence/` directory remained empty\n* `qc/index.json` still shows step_2 as `running`\n\n## Expected behavior\n\n1. **Detect transient network failure** — distinguish sustained connectivity loss from individual API errors\n2. **Write error back to job record** — set `state=failed`, `error_code`, `error_message` so the failure is visible\n3. **Update QC index** — mark the step as `failed` with a reason\n4. **Allow retry** — leave the job in a retryable state so a subsequent worker pickup or manual re-trigger can resume\n\n## Additional context\n\n* The `http_client` retry logic (4 attempts with backoff) handles individual request failures, but there is no circuit-breaker or global network-health check that detects when *all* requests are failing simultaneously\n* The LLM cache was also corrupted on startup (separate issue, recovered via fallback)\n* API logger has an invalid `datefmt` producing stderr spam (minor, separate issue)","priority":{"value":0,"name":"No [{"body":"## What this is\n\nPorts Lane 4 of the SPI-238 porting epic. The API on `dev` currently has bare-bones job submission and a single shared API key. This PR adds three capabilities from dev_sprint:\n\nMulti-user authentication via a TOML-based user registry, replacing the single `ELIJAH_API_KEY` model while preserving backward compatibility. End-to-end run-config propagation so the run configuration (model set, behavior flags) flows from API request through the job queue row into worker dispatch and pipeline execution. Worker context helpers so workers can resolve which config and model set to use for a given job.\n\nFS12 route registration was intentionally deferred — the route file imports `packages.domain.fs12` which does not exist on `dev`. Tracked as SPI-239 under a separate \"Port FS12 Pricing Diagnostics to dev\" epic, with SPI-240 documenting the skip in SPI-238.\n\n## Technical detail\n\n### Summary\n\n9 files changed: 3 new modules (`services/api/auth.py`, `services/worker/run_config_context.py`, `tests/test_api_auth.py`), 6 modified (`core/run_config.py`, `services/api/app.py`, `services/api/job_service.py`, `services/api/worker_dispatch.py`, `services/api/routes/jobs.py`, `services/api/mappers.py`).\n\n### Approach\n\n- **Auth**: New `auth.py` with `User`, `UserRegistry`, TOML loader, and `get_current_user` FastAPI dependency. Routes still use the existing `require_api_key`; the new registry is loaded onto `app.state` for sub-PR 2+ to wire per-user rate limits.\n- **Run-config propagation**: `job_service._resolve_run_config_for_submission()` creates or loads a DB-backed run config, threading `run_config_id`/`run_config_hash`/`model_set` into `JobRequestContext`. `worker_dispatch` loads the snapshot from DB and wraps pipeline calls in `model_set_context`.\n- **core/run_config.py gap**: 3 DB-backed functions (`create_run_research_run_config`, `load_run_research_run_config`, `build_run_config_execution_snapshot`) plus `RUN_RESEARCH_BEHAVIOR_KEYS` were ported as a dependency gap between SPI-225 and this ticket.\n- **mappers.py**: Only the `worker_payload_to_job_result` run_config/llm_config field additions. FS1 `target_set_integrity` changes were excluded (not SPI-236 scope).\n\n### Tests\n\n- `tests/test_api_auth.py` — 24 tests covering UserRegistry primitives, TOML loading/validation, fallback, `get_current_user` dependency, and app factory wiring.\n- `tests/test_job_queue_artifacts.py` — existing regression (identical on both branches, 8 tests).\n- `tests/test_run_detail_api.py` — existing regression (identical on both branches, 6 tests).\n- `tests/test_api_service.py` — existing integration tests (30 tests).\n\n### Verification\n\n- [x] `uv run ruff check .`\n- [x] `uv run ruff format --check .`\n- [x] `uv run python -m pytest --ignore=tests/frontend -x -q` (988 passed, 1 pre-existing flaky timing test)\n- [x] `uv run python -m pytest tests/test_api_auth.py -q` (24 passed)\n- [x] `uv run python -m pytest tests/test_job_queue_artifacts.py tests/test_run_detail_api.py tests/test_api_service.py -q` (44 passed)\n\n### Risks\n\n- `core/run_config.py` changes renamed `model_overrides` → `model_set` in `RUN_RESEARCH_DEFAULTS` and `merge_run_research_settings`. Any code that reads `model_overrides` from merged settings will get `None` instead of `{}`. All current tests pass.\n- `worker_dispatch.py` now passes `model_set`, `run_config_id`, `run_config_snapshot` kwargs to pipeline functions. Pipeline functions must accept `**kwargs` or these specific params. Existing pipelines handle this via `**kwargs`.\n\n### Branch-merge\n\nBranch `port/spi-236-api-auth-run-config-worker-context` based on `dev`. No merge conflicts.\n\n### Out of scope (deliberately)\n\n- `services/api/routes/fs12.py` — blocked on `packages/domain/fs12` not existing on dev (tracked as SPI-239)\n- `services/api/routes/driver_promotion.py` edge type comparison fix (bug fix, not SPI-236)\n- `services/api/mappers.py` `target_set_integrity` changes (FS1 scope)\n- `packages/storage/database.py` Alembic migration infrastructure\n- Route-level auth enforcement with `get_current_user` (sub-PR 2+)\n- `submitted_by` propagation to `JobRequestContext` / `JobRow` (sub-PR 4)\n- SPI-240 documents the FS12 deferral in the SPI-238 epic\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)","number":184,"title":"SPI-236: Port API auth, run-config propagation, and worker context"},{"body":"## What this is\n\nToday the API has one shared key (`ELIJAH_API_KEY`) — every caller is anonymous, and there's no way to scope rate limits, cost caps, or audit trails per user. The 10-user end state needs real multi-tenant identity: every caller gets their own key, and downstream layers (rate limits, cost caps, audit, run attribution) can scope on the resolved user.\n\nThis PR is sub-PR 1 of issue #151: the **identity foundation**. It introduces a per-user registry loaded from `config/api_users.toml`, exposes the resolved `User` to every request via `request.state.user`, and keeps the existing single-key flow working as a fallback so there's no rolling-deploy risk.\n\nThe visible behaviour change: with the toml file present, each caller's API key resolves to a stable `user_id` that downstream sub-PRs key off. With the file absent, behaviour is identical to today.\n\n## Technical detail\n\n### Summary\n\n- New `services/api/auth.py` with `User`, `UserRegistry`, `load_user_registry`, `get_user_registry`, `get_current_user`.\n- `app.state.user_registry` is built at startup from `config/api_users.toml` (or env override `ELIJAH_API_USERS_PATH`), falling back to a single-user registry built from `ELIJAH_API_KEY`.\n- `config/api_users.example.toml` ships as a checked-in template; the real file is gitignored.\n- 24 new tests covering the registry primitives, path resolution, validation errors, and the FastAPI dependency.\n\n### Approach\n\nThe `UserRegistry` stores `dict[api_key, User]` and resolves keys via `secrets.compare_digest` against every entry — constant-time to avoid timing leaks of valid keys. Loading is permissive on operator misconfig (corrupt toml, duplicate user_id/api_key, missing fields all log a warning and fall back to `ELIJAH_API_KEY`) so a typo in `api_users.toml` never wedges deployment.\n\n`get_current_user` returns `None` when the registry is empty (matches today's no-key dev mode), raises 401 when a key is supplied that doesn't resolve, and raises 401 when the registry is populated but no key was supplied. Resolved users are attached to `request.state.user` so middleware in subsequent sub-PRs can read them without re-running resolution.\n\nThe existing routes that depend on `require_api_key` still work unchanged — this PR adds the foundation but doesn't yet swap any callers over. Sub-PR 2 will add the per-user rate-limit key function; sub-PR 3 the per-user cost caps; sub-PR 4 the `submitted_by` propagation.\n\n### Tests\n\n`tests/test_api_auth.py` — 24 tests:\n\n- `TestUserRegistryPrimitives` (6) — empty, lookup with empty/unknown/valid key, multiple users, n_users counts distinct user_ids\n- `TestLoadUserRegistryFallback` (7) — no toml → empty / fallback key → single-user, explicit path, env-var override, default path discovery, corrupt toml → warning + fallback, metadata preservation\n- `TestLoadUserRegistryValidation` (4) — duplicate api_key, duplicate user_id, missing api_key, missing user_id all warn + fall back\n- `TestGetCurrentUserDependency` (5) — empty-registry None, missing-key 401, invalid-key 401, valid-key resolution, request.state.user attachment\n- `TestAppFactoryWiresRegistry` (2) — `create_app` attaches the registry in fallback mode and multi-user mode\n\n### Verification\n\n- [x] `pytest tests/test_api_auth.py` (24 passed)\n- [x] `pytest tests/` (1572 passed, 5 skipped, 0 failed)\n- [x] `ruff check .`\n- [x] `ruff format --check services/api/auth.py services/api/app.py tests/test_api_auth.py`\n\n### Risks\n\nLow. The default-on path is the existing single-key fallback; no production deployment changes behaviour until `config/api_users.toml` is created. Operator misconfig (bad toml) logs a warning and reverts to `ELIJAH_API_KEY` rather than wedging startup.\n\nThe constant-time comparison is best-effort: it iterates every stored key on every request. With the target ~10 users that's still microsecond-level. If the registry grows past ~hundreds, swap to a hashed lookup with constant-time compare on the hash.\n\n### Branch-merge\n\nStacked on PR #157.\nBase branch for review: `patrick/149-polymarket-transformer`.\n\n### Out of scope (deliberately)\n\n- **Per-user rate-limit key function** — sub-PR 2 of #151.\n- **Per-user cost caps + audit attribution** — sub-PR 3 of #151.\n- **`submitted_by` propagation onto `JobRequestContext`** — sub-PR 4 of #151.\n- **Role enforcement** — `role` is informational today; later sub-PRs can layer per-role rate-limit / cost-cap policy.\n- **Wiring `get_current_user` into existing routes** — `require_api_key` still owns the bouncer-at-the-door responsibility; switching routes to consume `current_user` is a follow-up that doesn't change auth semantics.\n\ncc @kungfusaini","number":158,"title":"#151 - Cross-FS: per-user authentication + per-user rate limits + per-user cost caps: per-user auth foundation"},{"body":"## Summary\n- **API key auth**: Single shared `ELIJAH_API_KEY` env var, `X-API-Key` header, `secrets.compare_digest`. Router-level — zero changes to individual route signatures. Auth disabled when key is empty (dev mode).\n- **Rate limiting**: slowapi on 12 expensive POST endpoints (5/min for research jobs, LLM ops; 10/min for step-level operations). In-memory storage.\n- **Frontend**: `api.js` reads key from `sessionStorage`, sends `X-API-Key` on every request. Prompts on 401.\n- **Dev vs prod**: Dev = optional (logs warning). Prod = required (`compose.prod.yml` refuses to start without it).\n- **Docs**: Full rate limit table in CLAUDE.md, README.md auth section, new `docs/architecture/06-security.md` with auth flow, rate limits, and 3-phase roadmap.\n\nChained on: PR #22 (SPI-169)\n\n## Test plan\n- [x] 683 tests pass, 5 skipped, 0 failures\n- [x] mypy clean (329 files, 0 errors)\n- [x] ruff format + check clean\n- The encapsulation leak is a known follow-up (\\`repo.get_run_detail()\\`) — when that lands, both the route and this test can stop reaching into \\`_database\\`.\n\n## Test plan\n- [x] \\`uv run pytest tests/test_run_detail_api.py\\` — 6/6 pass\n- [x] ruff + ruff format clean\n- [ ] CI green\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)","number":104,"title":"tests: cover GET /api/runs/{run_id} (#98 follow-up)"},{"body":"## Summary\n\nAdds `POST /questions/draft` — backend endpoint that takes a user's free-text forecasting question draft (plus optional instructions) and returns a structured proposal: refined label, target type, mutually exclusive options, suggested close date, unit, and reasoning.\n\nThis is the backend half of FS1-2. The UI is wired up in PR3.\n\n## Target types\n\nThe LLM selects one of four target types, which map to the internal `QuestionType`:\n\n| Target type | Example options | Maps to |\n|---|---|---|\n| `binary` | `[\"Yes\", \"No\"]` | `binary` |\n| `categorical` | `[\"Labour\", \"Conservative\", ...]` | `categorical` |\n| `numeric_ordinal` | `[\"below 0%\", \"0-2%\", \"2-5%\", \"above 5%\"]` | `numeric_bracket` |\n| `time_ordinal` | `[\"before Jun 2026\", \"Jun-Dec 2026\", ...]` | `numeric_bracket` |\n\nThe endpoint runs the LLM output through `research.question_classification.infer_classification()` / `infer_unit()` / `infer_buckets()` as a sanity pass — this catches e.g. an LLM returning `categorical` for options that should reclassify as `numeric_bracket`.\n\n## Files changed\n\n**New:**\n- `services/worker/agents/QuestionDefAgent/question_drafter.py` — `QuestionDrafterTool(BaseLLMTool)` with `DraftedQuestion` Pydantic schema\n- `services/worker/agents/QuestionDefAgent/prompts/question_drafter_system.md` — role, target types, output schema\n- `services/worker/agents/QuestionDefAgent/prompts/question_drafter_user.md` — draft + instructions template\n- `tests/test_question_drafter.py` — 8 unit tests\n- `tests/test_api_question_draft.py` — 7 endpoint tests\n\n**Modified:**\n- `packages/contracts/questions.py` — `QuestionDraftRequest`, `QuestionDraftResponse` DTOs\n- `packages/contracts/__init__.py` — export new DTOs\n- `services/api/routes/questions.py` — `POST /questions/draft` endpoint, rate-limited 5/minute\n\n## Design choices\n\n- **Rate-limited 5/minute** — matches other LLM-expensive endpoints (e.g. domain guess, IW trigger)\n- **Inherits `require_api_key` auth** via router-level dependency in `app.py`\n- **Caching disabled** on the tool (`cache_enabled=False`) — user input varies widely, caching would only add noise\n- **Graceful fallback** on invalid close date: if the LLM returns malformed ISO, endpoint falls back to `today + 180d`\n- **Error handling** returns 502 `drafter_failed` if the LLM call throws, so the frontend can surface a clear error\n\n## Test plan\n\n- [x] `pytest tests/test_question_drafter.py -v` — 8/8 pass\n- [x] `pytest tests/test_api_question_draft.py -v` — 7/7 pass\n- [x] Full suite: `pytest tests/ -q` — only 3 pre-existing LLM-flaky baseline tests fail (same on `dev`)\n- [x] mypy clean, ruff clean\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)","number":66,"title":"FS1-2 - natural-language question drafting endpoint (PR 2)"},{"body":"## Summary\n\nThree latent bugs in the Paddy-Schema integration that block the live FS10 → Postgres path end-to-end. All surfaced by attempting the live calibration sweep for #106.\n\n### Bug 1 — Enum case mismatch (8 columns)\n\n`SQLAlchemy.Enum(PyEnum, native_enum=True)` defaults to serialising the Python member **name** (e.g. `BINARY`), but the Postgres ENUMs were created with lowercase **values** (`binary`). This produced\n\n\\`\\`\\`\npsycopg.errors.InvalidTextRepresentation: invalid input value for enum question_type: \\\"BINARY\\\"\n\\`\\`\\`\n\non the very first INSERT. Fixed by introducing a small `_pg_enum()` helper that sets `values_callable=lambda m: [e.value for e in m]`, and applying it to all 8 enum columns (`question_type`, `run_status`, `graph_status`, `node_type`, `edge_type`, `origin_kind` ×2, `reference_kind`).\n\n### Bug 2 — QuestionSet FK insert order\n\n`save_questions` and `save_graph_store_bundle` were `add_all`-ing `QuestionRow` *before* the parent `QuestionSetRow`. An autoflush triggered by `session.get(RunRow, ...)` flushed the children first, producing\n\n\\`\\`\\`\nForeignKeyViolation: insert or update on table \\\"questions\\\" violates foreign key constraint \\\"questions_question_set_id_fkey\\\"\nDETAIL:  Key (question_set_id)=(fs10_validation) is not present in table \\\"question_sets\\\".\n\\`\\`\\`\n\nFixed by reordering and adding explicit `session.flush()` between the parent and child `add_all`s.\n\n### Bug 3 — Empty-graph FK violation\n\nWhen FS10 ingest calls `flush_graph_persistence_state` after `register_questions_in_store`, the graph (\\\"fs10_validation\\\") exists but has no nodes yet. The existing run-synth loop synthesises a `RunRow` only when a node provides a `question_id`, so for the empty graph no run is created — but the graph row was still being inserted, producing\n\n\\`\\`\\`\nForeignKeyViolation: insert or update on table \\\"graphs\\\" violates foreign key constraint \\\"graphs_run_id_fkey\\\"\nDETAIL:  Key (run_id)=(fs10_validation) is not present in table \\\"runs\\\".\n\\`\\`\\`\n\nFixed by filtering `graphs` to only those with `node_ids` before SQL persistence — empty graphs get written on a later flush once nodes arrive.\n\n## Why draft\n\nThis unblocks the live FS10 calibration sweep for #106 but I haven't actually run the sweep through Docker yet (the auth path goes through the LiteLLM proxy in dev compose, separate setup). Will mark ready once the sweep runs cleanly inside the dev stack.\n\n## Test plan\n\n- [x] `tests/test_storage_repositories.py` + `tests/test_worker_storage_adapters.py` still pass (13 tests)\n- [ ] Live FS10 calibration sweep runs end-to-end through Docker without FK / enum errors\n- [ ] No regressions in batch / gjopen pipeline (graph persistence)\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)","number":113,"title":"storage: fix three Paddy-Schema bugs surfaced by FS10 calibration sweep"}]
```

> AGENT

Found it — PR #158 on dev_sprint has the original motivation written out clearly:

> "Today the API has one shared key (`ELIJAH_API_KEY`) — every caller is anonymous, and there's no way to scope rate limits, cost caps, or audit trails per user. The **10-user end state** needs real multi-tenant identity: every caller gets their own key, and downstream layers (rate limits, cost caps, audit, run attribution) can scope on the resolved user."

So the driving reasons were:

1. **Rate limits per user** — with a shared key you can't throttle heavy users without throttling everyone
2. **Cost caps per user** — no way to track or limit LLM spend by individual
3. **Audit / run attribution** — every job submission is anonymous today; with per-user identity you know who ran what
4. **The 10-user target** — there was an explicit target of ~10 named users, which is also why the TOML approach (rather than a full DB user table) was chosen — simple enough for that scale

The SPI-242 ticket we just created (CF ZT bridging) is essentially the browser-user path to the same destination.

> DEVELOPER

so kind of the way I see it is that if people are using this from the GUI, which is the attention like the online web app, this this this this this this this this this this this feature is kind of bullshit, it doesn't really do anything. And also like is this wired into like LLM at the moment? Like is that kinda sort of done right now? Like if I'm a user and I log into the GUI, can I see that jobs that are for me like I think this is only done at the API level, right?

> AGENT

Yeah, exactly right on both counts.

**It's only at the API key level, not the GUI level.** Right now if two people open the browser and use the app, they both share the same `ELIJAH_API_KEY` — the backend has no idea they're different people. The `UserRegistry` this PR adds resolves API keys to `User` objects, but the GUI never sends different keys per person. So for GUI users, this PR does nothing.

**It's not wired into anything downstream yet either.** The PR explicitly leaves these as "out of scope":
- Rate limits are still shared (sub-PR 2)
- Cost caps don't exist yet (sub-PR 3)  
- `submitted_by` isn't on job records yet (sub-PR 4)

So even for programmatic API callers with their own keys — the `User` object gets resolved and attached to `request.state.user`, but nothing reads it yet. It's infrastructure with no consumer.

**The honest summary:** this PR is purely foundational. It builds the identity layer so that sub-PRs 2/3/4 have something to key off. On its own, in your current GUI-only + CF ZT setup, it changes nothing observable.

The part that would actually make it meaningful for GUI users is SPI-242 (the CF ZT bridging) — that's what would make "who opened the browser" become a real identity that the rate limit and cost cap layers can use.

> DEVELOPER

Does DevSprint have this functionality if you check out the dem the De DevSprint feature the the diff that we're working off, does that have that functionality? I'm asking if we're leaving anything behind or not.

> AGENT

dev_sprint only has sub-PR 1. The evidence:

- `auth.py` on dev_sprint is 286 lines — same foundation we ported, nothing more
- `rate_limit.py` on dev_sprint still uses `get_remote_address` (IP-based, not per-user) — sub-PR 2 never landed
- No cost cap or `submitted_by` code in the commit log

**We're not leaving anything behind.** Sub-PRs 2, 3, and 4 were planned in the PR #158 body but never implemented on dev_sprint. The foundation (sub-PR 1) is all that exists, and that's exactly what this PR ports.

> DEVELOPER

Okay okay so you're telling me that right now there's just the API key per user scoping there's no wiring into like LLM to see how much each user is using all we have is different Eliza keys and that's on different runs. so I've seen this like sub PR2, sub PR, sub PR three, etc but like where are you getting this shit from?

> AGENT

From the PR #158 body on dev_sprint — the original author wrote it as a roadmap when they submitted the foundation. It was a plan, not completed work. Those sub-PRs were never opened, never merged, they're just text in a PR description.

So the actual state is: there is one API key per user, full stop. That's the only thing that exists. No rate limiting per user, no cost tracking per user, no job attribution. Just "here's a way to give different people different keys."

> DEVELOPER

Okay, so you need to assign some tickets then to me, you need to assign a ticket for you know wiring in this multi-use authentication from Cloudflare to the GUI and from the GUI into Light LLM and we need to we like we need to have this kind of shit integrated already, you know? yeah, that way we can put like the restrictions on people in terms of budget, you know? Or do we need to build our own like infrastructure for restricting like the number of runs or stuff like this? to think about because like let's say that I'm the admin. Do I just restrict by cost? Do I restrict by number of runs? How do we do that? So what happens to run if you run out of money in the middle?

> AGENT

Good questions first, then I'll make the tickets.

**Cost vs. run count:** Cost is the right primitive. Runs vary wildly in expense — a single full-pipeline run on 20 questions costs 10x a one-step run on 1 question. Capping by run count doesn't capture that. Cap by LLM spend.

**LiteLLM already does this.** LiteLLM has built-in per-key budget limits — you set a `max_budget` on a virtual key and it rejects calls when the budget is exceeded. You don't need to build your own spend tracking; you just need to wire the per-user identity through to LiteLLM so it knows which key to debit.

**What happens mid-run when budget is exhausted:** Right now the worker would get a 429/402 from LiteLLM, the LLM call would throw, and the job would die with an unhandled error. That needs to be caught explicitly and written back as a clean `budget_exceeded` job failure — not a crash.

---

So the real ticket stack is:

1. Bridge CF ZT email → Elijah `User` so GUI users have identity
2. Pass `user_id` on every LiteLLM call so spend is tracked per user
3. Wire LiteLLM virtual keys + budget limits to per-user caps
4. Catch budget-exceeded errors in the worker and fail jobs cleanly
5. `submitted_by` on job records so users only see their own jobs in the GUI

```json
{
  "title": "Bridge CF ZT email to per-user Elijah identity for GUI users",
  "team": "Spire_Elijah",
  "state": "Backlog",
  "assignee": "c7a8f74f-a7ba-41e8-b771-85fc97b42216",
  "priority": 2,
  "description": "## Problem\n\nGUI users authenticate via Cloudflare Zero Trust (email → CF session cookie) but the Elijah backend treats them all as the same anonymous shared key. There is no per-user identity at the API layer for browser users, so rate limits, cost caps, and job attribution cannot distinguish between them.\n\nCF ZT injects the authenticated user's email in the `Cf-Access-Authenticated-User-Email` request header on every request that passes through the tunnel.\n\n## Goal\n\nRead `Cf-Access-Authenticated-User-Email` in `get_current_user` (`services/api/auth.py`) as a second identity path alongside `X-API-Key`. Map the email to a `User` object so downstream layers (budget caps, job attribution) can scope on it.\n\n## Approach options\n\n1. **Header bridging (simplest)** — read the CF header, look up email in a config table (TOML or DB), return a `User`. No CF config changes needed.\n2. **Auto-issued per-user keys** — on first login, generate and persist a personal API key for the user, inject it into the HTML instead of the shared key.\n3. **CF Access JWT verification** — verify the full CF JWT for cryptographic proof, derive `User` from claims.\n\nOption 1 is the recommended starting point given the current ~10-user scale.\n\n## Extension point\n\n`get_current_user` in `services/api/auth.py` is the right place. Both paths (CF header and `X-API-Key`) should resolve to the same `User` object so budget caps and job attribution work identically.\n\n## Related\n\n- SPI-242 (exploration ticket)\n- Blocked by: this ticket must land before per-user budget caps or job attribution are meaningful for GUI users\n"
}
```

```json
  "description": "## Problem\n\nEvery LLM call goes through LiteLLM but carries no user identity. LiteLLM supports a `user` field on API calls that it uses to track spend per user and enforce per-user budget limits — but we never set it, so all spend rolls up anonymously.\n\n## Goal\n\nThread `user_id` from the resolved `User` object (available on `request.state.user` after SPI-243 lands) down to every LiteLLM call made during a job. LiteLLM will then attribute spend correctly and can enforce per-key budget limits.\n\n## Approach\n\nLiteLLM's `user` parameter maps to virtual key spend tracking. The `user_id` needs to travel from the API request → `JobRequestContext` → worker dispatch → every `litellm.completion()` / `litellm.acompletion()` call site.\n\nThe model_set_context / ContextVar pattern already used for model routing is the right analogue — a `user_context` ContextVar set at job dispatch time, read by the LLM client wrapper before each call.\n\n## Dependency\n\nRequires SPI-243 (CF ZT identity bridging) so GUI users have a real `user_id` to pass.\n\n## Related\n\n- SPI-243 (identity bridging — must land first)\n- SPI-245 (budget cap enforcement — depends on this)\n",
  "blockedBy": [
    "SPI-243"
  ]
}
```

```json
  "description": "## Problem\n\nThere is no mechanism to limit how much LLM spend a given user can consume. A single user running a large batch job can exhaust the entire LiteLLM budget. If a budget is hit mid-run, LiteLLM returns a 429/402, the worker throws an unhandled error, and the job dies with no useful error message.\n\n## Goal\n\n1. **Configure per-user budget limits** in LiteLLM (virtual key `max_budget` per user).\n2. **Catch budget-exceeded errors** in the worker and write back a clean `budget_exceeded` job failure — not a crash. The job record should get `state=failed`, `error_code=budget_exceeded`, and a meaningful `error_message`.\n3. **Admin visibility** — some lightweight way for the admin to see current spend per user against their limit (LiteLLM's `/spend` endpoint or dashboard).\n\n## Design questions\n\n- Budget period: per-month rolling, per-calendar-month, or lifetime? Monthly rolling is simplest.\n- What should happen to in-flight steps when budget is hit — abort immediately or finish the current step and stop before the next?\n- Should the frontend show a \"budget exhausted\" state to the user, or just a generic failure?\n\n## Dependency\n\nRequires SPI-244 (user_id passed to LiteLLM) so spend is attributed correctly before limits can be enforced.\n\n## Related\n\n- SPI-243 (identity bridging)\n- SPI-244 (user_id threading to LiteLLM — must land first)\n",
  "blockedBy": [
    "SPI-244"
  ]
}
```

```
1	"""Per-user authentication foundation for the elijah API (issue #151 sub-PR 1).
2	
3	Replaces the single-shared-key model (one ``ELIJAH_API_KEY`` for everyone)
4	with a multi-user identity registry: each user has a name, an API key,
5	and optional metadata. The middleware resolves the inbound API key to a
6	``User`` record and attaches it to ``request.state.user`` so routes /
7	rate-limiters / cost-cap layers can scope on the resolved identity.
8	
9	Backward-compat by design: when no ``config/api_users.toml`` is present,
10	the registry falls back to ``ELIJAH_API_KEY`` (resolved as a single
11	anonymous user named ``"shared"``). Existing single-key deployments
12	continue working unchanged; multi-user deployments add the toml file
13	to enable per-user identity.
14	
15	Sub-PR 1 scope (this PR):
16	
17	- Identity infrastructure: ``User`` dataclass, ``UserRegistry`` loader,
18	  ``get_current_user`` dependency.
19	- Backward-compat with the existing ``require_api_key`` flow.
20	
21	Out of scope (separate sub-PRs):
22	
23	- Per-user rate limit key function (sub-PR 2).
24	- Per-user cost caps + audit attribution (sub-PR 3).
25	- ``submitted_by`` propagation onto ``JobRequestContext`` (sub-PR 4).
26	"""
27	
28	from __future__ import annotations
```

```json
{
  "title": "Remove shared ELIJAH_API_KEY fallback once per-user identity lands",
  "team": "Spire_Elijah",
  "state": "Backlog",
  "assignee": "c7a8f74f-a7ba-41e8-b771-85fc97b42216",
  "priority": 3,
  "description": "## Problem\n\nThe current auth system has a fallback mode where a single shared `ELIJAH_API_KEY` is accepted and maps all callers to an anonymous `\"shared\"` user. This was a backward-compatibility shim to avoid breaking existing deployments while per-user identity was being built. Once every caller (GUI users via CF ZT, programmatic callers via `api_users.toml`) has a real identity, the shared key fallback should be removed.\n\n## Goal\n\n- Remove `fallback_mode` from `UserRegistry` and `load_user_registry`\n- Remove `ELIJAH_API_KEY` as an auth mechanism (it can stay for other config purposes if needed, but should no longer act as an API key)\n- Require either a valid `X-API-Key` from `api_users.toml` or a CF ZT identity — no anonymous fallback\n- Update startup logging and error messages accordingly\n\n## Dependency\n\nMust not land until SPI-243 (CF ZT identity bridging) is complete. Removing the fallback before every user has a real identity path would lock everyone out.\n\n## Related\n\n- SPI-243 (CF ZT identity — must land first)\n",
  "blockedBy": [
    "SPI-243"
  ]
}
```

what is teh # Default location for the multi-user registry. Caller-overrideable via
# ``ELIJAH_API_USERS_PATH`` env var so tests / staging can use isolated
# files. The shipped repo carries ``config/api_users.example.toml`` as a
# template; real key files are gitignored.
_DEFAULT_USERS_PATH = Path("config/api_users.toml")
_USERS_PATH_ENV = "ELIJAH_API_USERS_PATH"
 for ?

> AGENT

That comment is explaining three things:

1. **Default path** — if you don't configure anything, the app looks for `config/api_users.toml` relative to the working directory (i.e. the repo root) to find the user registry.

2. **`ELIJAH_API_USERS_PATH` env var** — you can override that default path by setting this env var, pointing at any TOML file you want. Tests use this to point at temp files instead of the real config, and staging could point at a different file from prod.

3. **The example template** — `config/api_users.example.toml` is checked into the repo so people know the format. The actual `config/api_users.toml` with real API keys is gitignored so secrets don't get committed.

Short version: it's just the plumbing for "where do we find the user list, and how can it be overridden per-environment."

```
"""
Artifact download routes for the API service.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from fastapi import APIRouter, Depends, HTTPException, status
from fastapi.responses import FileResponse

from packages.contracts import ApiError

from ..dependencies import get_job_service
from ..job_service import JobService

router = APIRouter()


@router.get("/artifacts/{artifact_id}")
def download_artifact(
    artifact_id: str,
    job_service: JobService = Depends(get_job_service),
) -> FileResponse:
    artifact = job_service.resolve_artifact(artifact_id)
    if artifact is None or not artifact.path.exists() or not artifact.path.is_file():
        raise HTTPException(
            status_code=status.HTTP_404_NOT_FOUND,
            detail=ApiError(code="artifact_not_found", message=f"Unknown artifact: {artifact_id}").model_dump(),
        )
    return FileResponse(
        artifact.path,
        media_type=artifact.content_type,
        filename=artifact.path.name,
    )


__all__ = ["router"]
```

```json
{
  "command": "cd /Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah && gh pr create --title \"Fix ArtifactBytesMissingError propagating as 500 in artifact download\" --base dev --draft --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## What this is\n\nWhen artifact bytes are missing from object storage, `job_service.resolve_artifact` raises `ArtifactBytesMissingError`. Without this fix, that exception propagates unhandled through the download route and surfaces as a 500. This PR catches it and returns a clean 404 with `error_code: artifact_bytes_missing`.\n\nThe error class was added in SPI-236 (PR #184) but the route-level catch was missed.\n\n## Technical detail\n\n### Summary\n\nOne file changed: `services/api/routes/artifacts.py` — import `ArtifactBytesMissingError` and wrap `resolve_artifact` in a try/except.\n\n### Approach\n\nDirect port of the dev_sprint behaviour for this route.\n\n### Tests\n\nExisting `tests/test_job_queue_artifacts.py` covers the artifact resolution path. Yes, PR #187 for the `artifacts.py` fix. Want me to close it and just include it in another way, or is it fine?
```

> DEVELOPER

what even is that?

> AGENT

When we pulled the latest dev_sprint and compared it to dev, we found one thing directly related to SPI-236 that got missed: `routes/artifacts.py` wasn't catching `ArtifactBytesMissingError`. That error class was added in our PR, but without the catch in the route it would surface as a 500 instead of a 404 when artifact bytes are missing from storage.

It's a one-line fix — just wrapping `resolve_artifact` in a try/except. I opened PR #187 for it.

> AGENT

Interesting — `driver_promotion.py` on dev still has `edge.type == "CONDITIONAL"` (the plain comparison). The dev_sprint version changed it to `str(edge.type or "").strip().upper() == "CONDITIONAL"`. But that's actually the **defensive string wrapping anti-pattern** we've been cleaning up — `edge.type` is typed, so `None == "CONDITIONAL"` is already safe and returns `False`. SPI-232 correctly left it alone.

So the full picture:

- **`driver_promotion.py`** → SPI-232 is Done, dev is correct. The dev_sprint version introduced an anti-pattern — don't port it.
- **`graphs.py`** → Tracked in SPI-228, PR #186 is open draft (In Review on Linear). Covered.
- **`litellm_check.py`** → Will land in SPI-237 (Lane 5, not started yet). Covered.

Nothing is falling through the cracks.
